�鎖�� Conviction - The Meaning of Belief, and the Parallels Between Investing and Life We Build Ourselves
Inspired by P窶儁i Tiwa Shintadapong

When people hear the word Value Investor (VI), they often imagine someone buried in financial statements, analyzing numbers, meeting management, taking notes during investor calls.
But that窶冱 only one side of the story.
Because deep down, every investor knows this truth,
no matter how much you study, how many charts you analyze, or how well you understand the economy,
there is never a guarantee of success.
The market, like life, doesn窶冲 follow a fixed formula.
Being an entrepreneur is no different.
You can窶冲 know everything in advance.
That窶冱 why people often say: to build your own brand, you need both logic and gut feeling.
Reason gives you structure, but intuition gives you movement.

During our last Flow窶冱 Table session,
P窶� Mi shared something that resonated deeply with everyone at the table:
窶彜ometimes we know too much... that we end up being too afraid.
Even with the stocks we understand best, we invest too little,
and miss the opportunities that could change our lives.窶�
The room went quiet.
A simple sentence, but it carried weight.
The more we know, the more risk we see.
The more risk we see, the more we hesitate.
We build all the reasons in the world not to act.
But on the other hand, not knowing anything can be just as dangerous.
In the end, it窶冱 not about how much we know,
but whether we have the courage to believe in what we already know.
窶弋he ones who win in the market aren窶冲 those who know the most,
but those who believe the most, especially when no one else does.窶�

�嶋 Conviction = Faith supported by reason
P窶� Mi laughed as he said,
窶彜ometimes the stocks that suit us best窶ｦ might even share our name! 555窶�
He gave an example - Xiaomi.
It窶冱 a company he truly admires, not just because it窶冱 successful,
but because he believes in its vision and its R&D spirit,
its ability to keep innovating across smartphones, IoT, AI, and Smart Home technologies.
It窶冱 not just good at selling; it thinks deeply and sees far ahead.
Then he joked,
窶懌蝋iao窶� in Isaan dialect means 窶惑riend,窶� and 窶弄i窶� is my name,
so together it means 窶弄i窶冱 Friend!窶� 555窶�
Everyone laughed, but we all knew - there was truth beneath the joke.
That belief didn窶冲 come from coincidence or nameplay;
it came from experience, years of hard work, and deep understanding.
Because conviction doesn窶冲 come from luck, it窶冱 built, slowly, through sweat and clarity.

�穴 Conviction in Life is No Different from Conviction in the Market
Many of us already know what we should be doing,
but we don窶冲 start, because we窶决e afraid of failing.
We come up with every reason - money, timing, people,
but forget that the only reason we need to start is this:
we still believe.
Some people already have amazing ideas, great communities, and strong supporters,
but they never take action, like selling a good stock too early before it rallies.

�櫨 Conviction Mindset - Living with Belief in a Structured Way
1. Study deeply, then listen inward.
Knowledge is vital, but intuition matters too.
Don窶冲 let numbers drown out the quiet sense that comes from real understanding.
If you窶况e studied enough, your inner voice will tell you whether you窶决e on the right path.
And how do you tell the difference between bias, ego, and true intuition?
There窶冱 no textbook answer. You learn by observing, reflecting, and listening often.
Some people feel it in their thoughts, others in their body sensations,
each of us has our own way of perceiving intuition.
Find yours, and blend it with your logic, that窶冱 where conviction forms.

2. Stay calm with uncertainty, and accept it consciously.
Neither the stock market nor life is ever 100% safe.
Forecasting the market, as most investors agree, is almost pointless.
Instead, focus on what you can control,
specific actions that move the needle.
In business, that could mean improving your marketing plan,
or building a better recruitment system for your team.
You can窶冲 control the macro picture,
but you can always control how you respond to it.

3. Make peace with mistakes, but never lose faith.
Every mistake is interest paid on your experience.
It窶冱 the compounding effect of growth.
The first failure teaches one lesson.
The second combines both lessons, making 1 + 1 > 2.
Mistakes that don窶冲 kill you, strengthen you.
Life窶冱 graph isn窶冲 a straight line, it窶冱 a curve of ups and downs, like any long-term stock.
But when we learn from each dip, the learning curve grows exponentially, not linearly.
Eventually, that knowledge lifts your entire life chart upward.
True conviction doesn窶冲 come from always being right -
it comes from believing deeply enough to stay in the game, even after being wrong.

4. Surround yourself with people who believe in their own path.
Belief is contagious.
As research says, we are the average of the people we spend time with.
Stay close to people who think positively, who lift you when you窶决e tired.
And stay close to those who are skilled yet humble,
they often carry wisdom you won窶冲 find in any book.
Choose to be around people who genuinely wish you well,
because authenticity amplifies conviction.

5. Small Wins Matter.
Big goals can feel overwhelming.
Break them down into tiny milestones and celebrate each one.
For example, we want Flow窶冱 Table to reach 10,000 followers by the end of the year.
That窶冱 ambitious, there are many variables,
including whether our writing is even good enough yet (555).
But we just hit our first 1,000 followers,
and that窶冱 worth celebrating.
No big announcement, just a quiet moment of joy:
telling our friends, buying a small gift for ourselves, and smiling before we write the next post.
These small wins, stacked together, eventually build the big picture we dreamed of.

�町 Someone once said:
窶廣I can think faster than us,
but it can never believe the way humans do.窶�
AI learns from data.
Humans believe from instinct.
And that窶冱 our greatest edge,
the courage to believe in something before it exists.
That belief - raw, irrational, and deeply human,
is what creates everything new in this world.

�ｧｭ Takeaway from Flow窶冱 Table:
Conviction doesn窶冲 tell us to believe everything.
It reminds us to believe deeply in what we know is valuable.
Because life, like the market, never guarantees direction,
but we can always choose our action.

�璽 Bottom Fishing 窶� Turning Crisis into Opportunity
Inspired by P窶價ekky Suthon Singhasitthangkul

P窶價acky 窶� a well-known contrarian investor in Thailand 窶� has a signature move:
Bottom Fishing 窶� buying stocks that have fallen 80窶�90%, when almost everyone else has given up.
Why? Because he believes that some businesses are down, but not dead.

Bottom Fishing is one of the hardest strategies in investing.
The risk is high, the margin for error small, and conviction must be unshakable.
Throughout his entire career, P窶價acky found only two true winners through this method 窶�
Silo, a hospital stock that made a strong turnaround, and FRT, a retail company in Vietnam that pivoted into the pharmacy business.

Many others never made it back.
Sometimes what looks like the bottom窶ｦ can still go lower.
Sometimes the fall means permanent damage.

But sometimes 窶� just sometimes 窶� a company that still has life, leadership, and structure left窶ｦ rises again.
And that窶冱 where the golden opportunities hide.

�璽 Bottom Fishing isn窶冲 about buying the cheapest.
It窶冱 about having the courage to bet on something that窶冱 not dead yet.

P窶價acky窶冱 principle combines both belief and logic.
When the crowd panics, he digs deeper 窶�
asking, 窶廛oes this business still have breath? Can it turn around?窶�

A stock crashing 90% doesn窶冲 always mean it窶冱 over.
More often, it窶冱 fear 窶� and the market窶冱 inability to see true value 窶� that creates the dip.
That窶冱 where brave conviction meets opportunity.

�庁 In his entire journey, P窶價acky found only two stocks worth betting on during the storm:
窶｢ FRT 窶� a retail company in Vietnam that made a bold pivot into the pharmaceutical market, creating a new growth engine despite massive early losses.

People who follow this mindset must be willing to 窶徭tand in the dark,窶� not knowing when 窶� or if 窶� the light will come. �倦

�穴 Bottom Fishing in Life: When the Lowest Point Isn窶冲 the End, But a Reset

Many people think bottom = over.
But in truth, the lowest point of life is often the moment that forces us to pause 窶� to look inward and redefine ourselves.

To ask: Who am I, really? What am I good at? What still matters to me?

If we still have our core values, we窶决e not finished.

窶｢ Skills that the world still needs �ｧｩ
窶｢ A trusted network of people who believe in us �､�
窶｢ A heart that still wants to learn and grow �眺

That窶冱 not failure. That窶冱 consolidation 窶� the quiet period before a stronger rally.

�櫨 The Turnaround Playbook: How to Rebuild from Zero

1. Strengthen your roots (Core Focus)
Good businesses don窶冲 survive because of price, but because their roots hold strong.
Ask yourself: What real value do I offer the world?
Focus your energy there first.
For example, if your gift is branding or storytelling, use it to help small local businesses who have great products but lack marketing knowledge. That窶冱 how you rebuild momentum 窶� by giving your strength a purpose.

2. Extend your runway
When life hits hard, sometimes you just need time 窶� to rest, to reset, to breathe again.
Don窶冲 rush your recovery. Some people bounce back fast, others slower.
When it窶冱 time to rise, you窶冤l know it intuitively.

3. Don窶冲 recover alone
A Trusted Community is the best leverage.
Surround yourself with people who listen without judgment, who give real advice, and remind you that you窶决e not fighting your battles alone 窶� both outside and inside your head.

4. Build small, consistent catalysts
Momentum doesn窶冲 come from explosions.
It comes from small sparks repeated over time.
The first post. The first conversation. The first project.
One small act can change everything.
(Fun fact: Flow窶冱 Table started exactly like that 窶� from a small spark that grew naturally into something bigger.)

5. Always have a risk plan
Set time boundaries. Define what 窶徘rogress窶� means clearly.
If after 90 days there窶冱 no movement 窶� pivot.
Don窶冲 cling. Change the field, but keep the game going.

6. Fight. Then fight again.
Once you know you窶决e on the right path, go all in.
The best part of a comeback is discovering the new version of yourself 窶�
the one who dares to start again.

Even Flow窶冱 Table was born from that place 窶� a phase where something else ended, and we wanted to build a space that reignites energy, inspiration, and connection.

�圜 How to know if you窶决e really recovering:

�圸 Red Flags (still stuck)
窶｢ Talk a lot, act little
窶｢ Reject feedback or reality that challenges your view

笨� Green Flags (real turnaround)

窶｢ 窶彝eal people窶� show up to help without being asked
窶｢ You can stay calm and stick to the plan, even without immediate results

�ｧｭ In short:
Bottom Fishing in life isn窶冲 about buying low or selling high.
It窶冱 the art of recognizing your own value when no one else does.

�鎖�� Why a Trusted Community Matters for Your Career - Lessons from Fabrizio Romano, the Tier-1 Football Journalist 笞ｽ��

First of all, we窶囘 like to thank the owner of the TwentyTwo Diary channel for allowing us to write a reflection based on their video.
(The full link is in the comments below.) �剌

�･､ Yesterday, we shared real business cases from the F&B world - Yoguruto Thailand and Sappe Beauti - about how a good network can accelerate success.
Today, we窶囘 like to explore a case from the football world instead.
(The link to that article is also in the comments below.)

窶廩ere We Go!窶�
Anyone who watches football often knows this phrase well.
If Romano says it, you can be 90% sure a transfer is happening - even before any official club announcement.
There was even a player who once said he started packing his bags after seeing Romano窶冱 tweet.

�嶋 Romano first became known when writing for The Guardian, covering major transfer stories such as Paul Pogba窶冱 return to Manchester United.
But his fame skyrocketed after tweeting about Bruno Fernandes窶� transfer from Sporting Lisbon to United - a post that made fans everywhere wonder, what does 窶廩ere We Go窶� actually mean?

篋ｻ

1. Trust Comes Before Everything

In the beginning, Romano had no team - he did everything himself: graphics, research, sourcing, and outreach.
Especially when talking with insiders - agents, club owners, or staff - he valued trust above all else.
He even chose to skip major scoops, like Haaland窶冱 transfer to Man City, because honoring his word mattered more in the long run.
If a source didn窶冲 want something revealed, he kept it private.
Because once trust is broken, no one will ever share information again.

篋ｻ

2. The Power of a Wide Network

A single transfer involves a web of stakeholders - relatives, doctors, journalists, PR staff, social media teams, legal departments, and more.
The more people involved, the more likely information will flow.
Romano benefits from what we might call Human Intelligence 窶� a wide, interconnected web of people and relationships that keep him informed.

篋ｻ

3. The Right Place, the Right Person, the Right Timing

Romano works almost like the FBI - gathering clues, cross-checking facts, and connecting dots.
He doesn窶冲 rely on fancy tools - just his persistence and a single phone that窶冱 always on hand. �導
Each person might only know a small piece of the story, but Romano pieces them together logically until the big picture emerges.

篋ｻ

4. Relationships Before Business

Romano builds personal connections through simple gestures - buying someone coffee, chatting in lobbies, remembering small details about their lives.
People in football naturally love football, so they enjoy sharing what they know when they feel respected.
He also follows the principle of Reciprocal Effort - when someone helps him, he finds ways to give back:
giving public credit, promoting a small local team, or even treating them to a meal. �鎖��
It窶冱 all about creating an atmosphere where people want to work with you.

篋ｻ

�磁 The full video covers more fascinating details - how Romano gathers information, his methods, and the fine line between persistence and ethics.

笞��� Of course, we don窶冲 glorify him entirely.
There are grey ethical areas - this profession requires careful balance between work, people, and integrity.
Recently, he窶冱 faced criticism, such as alleged sponsorships from clubs for tweeting transfer news - raising questions about transparency.

篋ｻ

�升 What we truly take from this story is that having a trusted, high-quality community can genuinely build a career.
At Flow窶冱 Table, we窶决e not here to investigate rumors - and honestly, we don窶冲 need to! ��
But we believe that when good, capable people come together, willing to help one another, amazing things happen -
even without money involved. �､�

For example, people in our community often ask for introductions - like a restaurant owner looking for a marketing expert.
We may not always know someone directly, but we can ask around - 窶廩ey, do you know anyone who fits this?窶�
That small act of connection - no commission, no agenda - can create real value for everyone involved.

篋ｻ

And how has having (or lacking) a good community impacted your own career or life?
We窶囘 love to hear your thoughts in the comments. �剏�晶
